<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the sphere case of the closed-geodesic infinitude problem: every smooth Riemannian metric on the standard sphere <i>S</i><sup><i>n</i></sup>, <i>n</i> ≥ 2, has infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise distinct images. The two-sphere case is classical; the result in higher dimensions includes degenerate metrics. Using finite covers and theorems of Perelman and Rademacher–Taimanov, we also obtain the same conclusion for every smooth Riemannian metric on a nonempty closed smooth three-manifold.

- **claim-infinite-prime-geodesics-spheres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-spheres)): For every integer $n\ge2$ and every smooth Riemannian metric on the standard smooth sphere $S^n$, there are infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise different images.
  - Explanation: Every smooth Riemannian metric on a standard sphere of dimension at least two has infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise different images.
- **claim-finite-cover-corollary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-cover)): Let $N$ be a closed manifold admitting a finite smooth cover diffeomorphic to a standard sphere $S^n$, where $n\ge2$. Every smooth Riemannian metric on $N$ has infinitely many geometrically distinct prime closed geodesics.
  - Explanation: The infinitude of prime closed geodesic images passes to any closed manifold with a finite smooth cover diffeomorphic to a sphere.
- **claim-closed-three-manifolds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-three-manifolds)): Let $M$ be a nonempty closed smooth three-manifold, not necessarily orientable. Every smooth Riemannian metric on $M$ has infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise distinct images.
  - Explanation: Every smooth Riemannian metric on a nonempty closed smooth three-manifold has infinitely many prime closed geodesics with distinct images.
- **claim-analytic-foundations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-analytic)): The energy $E$ is smooth on $\Lambda=H^1(S^1,S^n)$, satisfies the Palais–Smale condition, and has precisely the smooth closed geodesics and the constants as critical points. There is a complete strong Riemannian metric on $\Lambda$, invariant under $G=O(2)$, for which a bounded normalized negative gradient of $E$ has a complete equivariant flow. Sufficiently small energy sublevels retract equivariantly to $M$.
  - Explanation: The energy on the loop space is smooth, satisfies Palais-Smale, and its critical points are the closed geodesics and constants; a complete equivariant normalized gradient flow exists.
- **claim-regular-strips** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-strips)): If a closed bounded energy interval contains no critical value, the gradient flow gives equivariant sublevel equivalences across it. At regular cuts, open and closed sublevel pairs compute the same equivariant cohomology, naturally for inclusions and fixed loci.
  - Explanation: Across an energy interval without critical values the gradient flow gives equivariant equivalences of sublevels.
- **claim-polygon-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-polygons)): Fix $B<\infty$. For every sufficiently large integer $N$, the energy sublevel $\{E\le B^2\}$ retracts onto its broken-geodesic polygons with $N$ equal time cells. The retraction preserves every smaller energy sublevel. It commutes with reflection and all time shifts preserving the vertex grid, hence with $K$ when $N$ is even. Regular polygon sublevels through this bound are compact smooth manifolds with boundary. At every critical point in the band, polygon and Hilbert indices and nullities agree. Regular energy cuts are dense in the band, and their sublevels have finite CW models.
  - Explanation: For fine enough subdivisions, energy sublevels retract onto broken-geodesic polygon models that respect the symmetries and preserve indices and nullities.
- **claim-prime-images** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-prime-images)): On any smooth Riemannian manifold, every nonconstant closed geodesic is an iterate of a prime geodesic. Two prime geodesics with the same image differ by phase and possibly reversal. Reversal never preserves an oriented phase orbit.
  - Explanation: Nonconstant closed geodesics are iterates of prime ones, and primes with the same image differ only by phase and reversal.
- **claim-index-and-root-types** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-local-iteration)): For each prime $c_i$, there is a number $\Delta_i\ge0$ such that $$\label{eq:local-index} \|I_{i,m+k}-I_{i,m}-I_{i,k}\|\le n,\qquad \|I_{i,m}-m\Delta_i\|\le n.$$ There is an integer $D_i\ge1$ such that, for $s=\gcd(m,D_i)$, every null field at $c_i^m$ repeats a null field at $c_i^s$. Thus only finitely many root kernels occur. For even $m$, $$\label{eq:local-even-index} \|I_{i,m}-2I_{i,m/2}\|\le n.$$
  - Explanation: The Morse index of iterates is almost additive with a mean index slope, and only finitely many root kernels occur.
- **claim-flow-lenses** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-lens)): Let a compact group $H$ act on a finite-dimensional or Hilbert manifold, and let $f$ be a smooth $H$-invariant function with a complete bounded $H$-equivariant strict descending flow $\phi$. Suppose the critical set in a closed energy band is a finite disjoint union of compact sets at a single level $c$, and that $H$ permutes these sets. Assume that the energy drop is bounded below away from every neighborhood of those sets, and that the corresponding assertion holds on the annuli of nested neighborhoods. These conditions hold for the normalized energy flow of Lemma <a href="#lem:local-analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-analytic">4</a>, and on compact polygon bands.* *For a common sufficiently large $T$ and sufficiently tight regular cuts $a<c<b$, set $$X=\{f\le b\},\quad X_-=\{f\le a\},\quad f_T=f\circ\phi_T,$$ $$\label{eq:local-lens} Y=\{x\in X:f_T(x)\le a\},\qquad N=\{x\in X:f_T(x)\ge a\},\qquad Q=N\cap Y.$$ There are natural relative cohomology isomorphisms, also after Borel construction, induced by $$\label{eq:local-lens-maps} (X,X_-)\longrightarrow(X,Y)\longleftarrow(N,Q).$$ The lens splits into clopen pairs in the chosen disjoint critical neighborhoods. All maps commute with restriction to a fixed submanifold when the same flow is restricted there, with subgroup restriction, and with cup product by ambient absolute classes.
  - Explanation: A common flow produces natural relative cohomology isomorphisms for crossings, compatible with fixed loci, subgroup restriction and cup products.
- **claim-uniform-local-control** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-local-control)): Assume there are finitely many prime images. There are constants $A,B<\infty$ such that every sufficiently tight crossing of a positive critical level has the following properties.* 1. *It decomposes into unoriented labels $(i,m)$ at that level. Let $\mathcal P_{i,m}$ be the ordinary pair for one orientation. Then $$\sum_j\dim H^j(\mathcal P_{i,m};\mathbb F_2)\le B,\qquad H^j(\mathcal P_{i,m};\mathbb F_2)=0 \quad\text{if }\|j-I_{i,m}\|>A.$$* 2. *Its reflection block is induced from $\mathcal P_{i,m}$; its $K$-block is induced from the $\langle\tau\rangle$-action on that pair. For odd $m$ the fixed label is empty. For even $m$, the ordinary fixed pair has total rank at most $B$ and support $\|j-I_{i,m/2}\|\le A$.* 3. *Full-to-fixed restriction respects the labels. These identifications are compatible with ambient maps and connecting homomorphisms.* 4. *Cup product by $e=\mathop{\mathrm{ev}}_0^*[S^n]^*\in H_R^n(\Lambda;\mathbb F_2)$ vanishes on the reflection crossing, including each individual label.* *The constants depend on the metric and the finite prime list, and include all degenerate iterates and all zero-mean-index primes.
  - Explanation: With finitely many prime images, local crossings have uniformly bounded rank and index support, and evaluation cup product vanishes on reflection crossings.
- **claim-normal-shift** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-local-shift)): Put $s=\gcd(m,D_i)$ and $k=m/s$. The ordinary local rank at $c_i^m$, in degree $j$, equals the ordinary local rank of the corresponding root label at $c_i^s$, in degree $j-(I_{i,m}-I_{i,s})$.
  - Explanation: The local rank at an iterate equals the local rank at its root label, shifted in degree by the index difference.
- **claim-arclength-homotopy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arclength)): Let $M$ be a compact Riemannian manifold. There is a jointly continuous map $$\Psi:[0,1]\times H^1([0,1],M)\longrightarrow H^1([0,1],M)$$ in the strong $H^1$ topology such that $\Psi_0$ is the identity, $\Psi_1\gamma$ has constant speed, and every $\Psi_u$ preserves length and endpoints. Constant paths and constant-speed paths are fixed. Moreover, $$\label{eq:shortening-energy} E(\Psi_u\gamma) \le (1-u)E(\gamma)+u\ell(\gamma)^2 \le E(\gamma).$$ The homotopy commutes with reversal. In particular, on loops it preserves evaluation at zero and the constant-loop subspace.
  - Explanation: A continuous strong-topology homotopy brings paths to constant speed, preserving length and endpoints and not increasing energy.
- **claim-local-path-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-shortening-recipes)): Fix $L_0<\infty$ and let $M$ be compact and simply connected. Pairs of paths with common endpoints and Lipschitz constants at most $L_0$ admit a finite cover by relative $C^0$ neighborhoods in this path-pair space with the following property. On each neighborhood there is a relative-endpoint homotopy from the first path to the second, continuous in the strong $H^1$ topology of the input paths and the homotopy parameter. Every path occurring in these homotopies has length at most a constant $K=K(M,L_0)$.
  - Explanation: Pairs of Lipschitz paths with common endpoints admit local homotopies whose paths have uniformly bounded length.
- **claim-shortening-block** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-shortening-block)): Let $M$ be compact and simply connected. There are constants $C,D>0$, depending only on $M$, with the following property. Suppose a path is divided into finitely many pieces, each of which, on a unit interval, has Lipschitz constant at most one. Near any such path, within this space of piecewise Lipschitz paths, there is a relative-endpoint homotopy to a path of length at most $D$. At every stage its length is at most its original length plus $C$. The homotopy is continuous in $H^1$ on that neighborhood. The constants are independent of the number of pieces.
  - Explanation: A long piecewise Lipschitz path can be shortened by a homotopy with bounded length overhead independent of the number of pieces.
- **claim-shortening-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-shortening-family)): Let $M$ be compact and simply connected. For each integer $j\ge0$ there are $a_j,H_j>0$ such that every $H^1$-continuous family $\Gamma:P\to\mathcal A_a$, where $P$ is a finite polyhedron of dimension at most $j$ and $a\ge a_j$, is homotopic to a family in $\mathcal A_{a-1}$ through $\mathcal A_{a+H_j}$. The constants do not depend on $P$ or on the number of its simplices.
  - Explanation: A finite-dimensional family of loops can be shortened by one unit with bounded overhead independent of the polyhedron.
- **claim-bounded-level-filling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-shortening-bounded)): For every $b>0$ and $j\ge0$ there is $R_{b,j}<\infty$ such that every finite $j$-cycle in $\mathcal A_b$ which is null-homologous in $\Lambda$ bounds a finite chain in $\mathcal A_{R_{b,j}}$.
  - Explanation: At a fixed bounded level, globally null cycles of a given degree bound chains below a finite level.
- **claim-fixed-degree-filling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-degree-death)): Let $M$ be compact and simply connected. For every integer $j\ge0$ there is $D_j>0$ such that, for every real $a>0$, each finite $j$-cycle in $\mathcal A_a$ which is null-homologous in $\Lambda$ bounds a finite chain in $\mathcal A_{a+D_j}$.
  - Explanation: In each fixed degree, globally null cycles fill with an additive length increment independent of the level.
- **claim-sphere-powers-resonance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sphere-powers)): There are constants $\alpha>0$ and $C_H$ depending on $g$ such that $$\label{eq:cj-resonance} \bigl\|k-\alpha\operatorname{cr}(z)\bigr\|\le C_H$$ for every nonzero homogeneous ordinary homology or cohomology class $z$ of degree $k$. For homology, $\operatorname{cr}$ is the infimum of the levels $a$ carrying the class in $X_a=\{F<a\}$; for cohomology it is the supremum of the levels on which its restriction vanishes. Moreover, each ordinary homology and cohomology group in a fixed degree has dimension at most one. There are classes $$\Theta\in H_{3n-2}(\Lambda),\qquad \omega\in H^{n-1}(\Lambda,M),\qquad \theta\in H^{3n-2}(\Lambda,M)$$ with $\langle\theta,\Theta\rangle=1$ such that, for every $m\ge1$, $$\begin{aligned} \|\Theta^{\bullet m}\|&=n+2md,& \|\omega^{\circledast m}\|&=(2m-1)d,\label{eq:cj-power-degrees}\\ \bigl\langle\theta\circledast\omega^{\circledast m}, \Theta^{\bullet(m+1)}\bigr\rangle&=1. \label{eq:cj-power-pairing} \end{aligned}$$ In particular these powers are nonzero, and $H_n(\Lambda)=\mathbb F_2\,[M]$.
  - Explanation: Resonance links degree and critical level on spheres, and powers of the loop-product and cohomology-product classes pair nontrivially.
- **claim-supported-cochain-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cj-small-chains)): Let $U$ be a pulled-back Thom cocycle, and let $h$ be a continuous map from its larger tube $O$ to a space carrying a cocycle $T$. Extend $h^*T$ by zero on simplices not wholly contained in $O$, and write $b=U\smile\widetilde{h^*T}$. Subdivide the finite parameter families factorwise so that a nonzero $U$-face forces its entire product rectangle into a fixed closed intermediate tube contained in $O$. Its preimage in each finite compact parameter domain is compact. Then $\delta b=0$ on the shuffled simplices and every later face or image-preserving comparison prism in these rectangles.* *If $T$ vanishes on loops of length less than $L$, the subdivision may also ensure the following: a contributing term with a nonzero $U$-face and a nonzero $T$-face has $\ell(h)\ge L-\varepsilon$ throughout its whole rectangle, for any fixed $\varepsilon>0$. The same conclusion holds for the block and skip faces used by cup-one, and for all later image-preserving subdivision prisms.
  - Explanation: Cup products of a Thom cocycle with a pulled-back cocycle extended by zero are cocycles on suitably subdivided small chains, with length control.
- **claim-cut-join-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cj-operations)): The cut has degree $-q$, takes absolute cycles to absolute cycles, and preserves global null-homology. For a cycle in $\mathcal A_a$ it is supported in $$\label{eq:cj-cut-bound} \mathcal A_{a-L+1}.$$ The same cut estimate holds on finite fillings. The join has degree $-n$ and satisfies the product boundary formula. On any prescribed finite collection of chains and fillings its radius can be chosen so that its output length is less than the sum of the two input length bounds plus $1$. All these statements are compatible with the refinement comparisons above.* *If $c$ is a fundamental cycle of constant loops, then $$\label{eq:cj-chain-unit} \mu(c,x)\simeq x\quad\text{in }\mathcal A_a$$ for every cycle $x$ in $\mathcal A_a$.
  - Explanation: The cut and join have the stated degrees, preserve cycles and null-homology, obey length bounds, and a constant-loop fundamental cycle acts as a unit for the join.
- **claim-filtered-interchange** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-filtered-interchange)): Let $T$ be a normalized cocycle of degree $p\ge1$ vanishing on $\mathcal A_L$, where $L>0$, and put $q=n+p-1$. Let $x$ and $y$ be arbitrary absolute cycles of respective degrees $j,N$ with $$j>n+p,\qquad N>n+p,$$ supported in $\mathcal A_a$ and $\mathcal A_b$. With sufficiently small join radius and adequate refinements, $$\label{eq:cj-interchange} \mu(D_Tx,y)\simeq\mu(D_Ty,x) \quad\text{in }\mathcal A_{a+b-L+c_{\rm cj}},\qquad c_{\rm cj}=4.$$ Both sides have degree $j+N-q-n$. The constant $c_{\rm cj}$ is independent of both degrees, the cocycle, and all length bounds. The join radius may be chosen for any additional prescribed finite collection of fillings without changing this constant.
  - Explanation: Cutting one factor and joining equals cutting the other and joining, up to a homology with a fixed additive length cost independent of the degrees.
- **claim-string-product-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cj-public-comparison)): The join $\mu$ induces $\bullet$ on ordinary homology. If $T$ represents a relative class $[T]\in H^p(\Lambda,M)$ and $b$ is a normalized relative cocycle of degree $r$, then for every absolute cycle $z$ of degree $p+r+n-1$, $$\label{eq:cj-scalar-comparison} \langle b,D_Tz\rangle =\langle[T]\circledast[b],[z]\rangle.$$ Here the brackets on the right also denote the image of $z$ in relative homology.
  - Explanation: The join induces the Chas-Sullivan product, and the cut pairs with relative classes via the Goresky-Hingston product.
- **claim-cut-of-power-is-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-absolute-unit)): For the choices $S,T,l,L$ in <a href="#eq:cj-thresholds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cj-thresholds">[eq:cj-thresholds]</a>, $$\label{eq:cj-absolute-unit} [D_TS]=[M]\quad\text{in }H_n(\Lambda).$$ Moreover $D_TS$ is supported in $\mathcal A_{l-L+1}$, and there is a constant $B$ independent of $m$ such that it is homologous to a fundamental constant-loop cycle in $\mathcal A_B$.
  - Explanation: The cut of the sphere power represents the fundamental class of the constants and is homologous to a constant-loop cycle within a level independent of the power.
- **claim-uniform-ordinary-death** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-ordinary-death)): For every smooth Riemannian metric on $S^n$, $n\ge3$, there is a constant $D>0$ such that, for every integer $j\ge0$ and every real $a>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \ker\bigl(H_j(X_a)\longrightarrow H_j(\Lambda)\bigr) &\subseteq \ker\bigl(H_j(X_a)\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D})\bigr), \label{eq:ordinary-absolute}\\ \ker\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\longrightarrow H_j(\Lambda,M)\bigr) &\subseteq \ker\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D},M)\bigr). \label{eq:ordinary-relative} \end{aligned}$$ The same constant works at regular and nonregular levels. No assumption on the number of prime geodesic images is required.
  - Explanation: A single filling increment works in every degree and at every level for ordinary homology, absolute and relative to constants.
- **claim-natural-gysin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gysin)): For every $G$-pair $(Y,A)$, there are natural exact sequences $$\begin{aligned} \cdots\longrightarrow H_G^{j-2}(Y,A) &\xrightarrow{\,U\,} H_G^j(Y,A) \xrightarrow{\mathop{\mathrm{res}}_R} H_R^j(Y,A) \xrightarrow{\int} H_G^{j-1}(Y,A) \xrightarrow{\,U\,} H_G^{j+1}(Y,A)\longrightarrow\cdots, \label{eqv:standard-gysin}\\ 0\longrightarrow H_G^j(Y,A) &\xrightarrow{\mathop{\mathrm{res}}_K}H_K^j(Y,A) \xrightarrow{\int}H_G^{j-1}(Y,A) \longrightarrow0. \label{eqv:weight-two-gysin} \end{aligned}$$ For an $R$-pair and a $K$-pair, respectively, the sign-line sequences are $$\begin{aligned} \cdots\longrightarrow H_R^{j-1}(Y,A) &\xrightarrow{\,w\,}H_R^j(Y,A) \longrightarrow H^j(Y,A) \longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A) \xrightarrow{\,w\,}H_R^{j+1}(Y,A)\longrightarrow\cdots, \label{eqv:reflection-gysin}\\ \cdots\longrightarrow H_K^{j-1}(Y,A) &\xrightarrow{\,v\,}H_K^j(Y,A) \longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A) \longrightarrow H_K^j(Y,A) \xrightarrow{\,v\,}H_K^{j+1}(Y,A)\longrightarrow\cdots. \label{eqv:half-shift-gysin} \end{aligned}$$ The maps commute with maps of pairs and subgroup restriction wherever the latter applies, and satisfy the projection formula for absolute base classes.
  - Explanation: Natural Gysin exact sequences relate Borel cohomology for the orthogonal group, reflection group and the four-group.
- **claim-global-generators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-global-generators)): For $n\ge3$, there are classes $$X_m\in B_\infty^{a_m}=H_R^{a_m}(\Lambda,M),\qquad m\ge1,$$ with the following properties.* 1. *The classes $$\xi_{m,k}=w^kX_m,\qquad \eta_{m,k}=e\,w^kX_m, \qquad m\ge1,\quad 0\le k\le d,$$ form a graded $\mathbb F_2$-basis of $B_\infty$. Their degrees are $$\|\xi_{m,k}\|=a_m+k,\qquad \|\eta_{m,k}\|=a_m+n+k.$$ We call the $\xi_{m,k}$ the *bottoms* and the $\eta_{m,k}$ the *tops* at stage $m$. Multiplication by $w^n$ annihilates $B_\infty$.* 2. *Every $X_m$ is the restriction of a class $\zeta_m\in H_G^{a_m}(\Lambda,M)$. If $m$ is odd, then $U^n\zeta_m=0$. If $m$ is even, there is also a class $Y_m\in H_G^{a_m+n}(\Lambda,M)$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{res}}_R Y_m=eX_m$.* 3. *For the round metric, let $Z_p$ be an open regular sublevel containing exactly the first $p$ positive critical manifolds, and choose $Z_0$ below the first one. For $H=R,G$, every crossing sequence for $(Z_p,Z_{m_0-1})$, $p\ge m_0$, is short exact. A chosen class of the $m$-th crossing has a unique extension to $H_H^j(\Lambda,Z_{m-1})$ whenever $j<a_{m+1}$, for $H=R,G$. The lifts in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> and the classes $X_m$ can be obtained from these relative extensions.
  - Explanation: Global reflection classes form a basis of bottoms and tops annihilated by a power of the sign class, with full-group lifts.
- **claim-round-attachment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eqv-round-attachment)): The negative bundle of $C_m$ has rank $a_m$, and the kernel of the round Hessian is exactly $TC_m$. For every closed subgroup $H\subset G$, the crossing has a Thom isomorphism $$H_H^j(Z_m,Z_{m-1})\cong H_H^{j-a_m}(C_m). \label{eqv:crossing-thom}$$ These isomorphisms commute with subgroup restriction and cup by absolute classes. The small sublevel $Z_0$ retracts $G$-equivariantly to $M$.
  - Explanation: For the round metric each crossing has a Thom isomorphism with the cohomology of the critical manifold, shifted by the negative bundle rank.
- **claim-critical-modules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eqv-critical-modules)): Define homogeneous polynomials $A_i\in\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $$A_0=1,\qquad A_1=w,\qquad A_i=wA_{i-1}+UA_{i-2}\quad(i\ge2). \label{eqv:grassmann-recurrence}$$ If $m$ is odd, the $G$-crossing is generated over $\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$, and $$A_{d+1}x_m=A_{d+2}x_m=0. \label{eqv:grassmann-relations}$$ If $m$ is even, multiplication by $U$ is injective on the $G$-crossing, and that crossing is generated over $\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$ and a chosen class $y_m$ of degree $a_m+n$ whose reflection restriction is $ex_m$.
  - Explanation: Odd crossings are cyclic modules with Grassmann relations; at even crossings the variable U acts injectively and two generators suffice.
- **claim-interval-collapse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eqv-interval-collapse)): Fix $m_0\ge1$. For $H=R,G$ and $p\ge m_0$, the crossing sequence is short exact: $$0\longrightarrow H_H^j(Z_p,Z_{p-1}) \longrightarrow H_H^j(Z_p,Z_{m_0-1}) \longrightarrow H_H^j(Z_{p-1},Z_{m_0-1}) \longrightarrow0. \label{eqv:interval-short-exact}$$ Restriction induces an isomorphism $$H_H^j(\Lambda,Z_{m-1}) \longrightarrow H_H^j(Z_m,Z_{m-1}) \qquad\text{if }j<a_{m+1}. \label{eqv:unique-relative-lift}$$
  - Explanation: Crossing sequences on round intervals are short exact and relative classes extend uniquely in low degrees.
- **claim-odd-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eqv-odd-torsion)): For every odd $m$ and every $n\ge3$, $$U^n\zeta_m=0.$$ When $n=3$, the supported relative lift also satisfies $$w^3\widetilde\zeta_m=0,\qquad U(w^2+U)\widetilde\zeta_m=0.$$
  - Explanation: Full-group lifts of odd bottoms are killed by a power of U, with extra relations when the dimension is three.
- **claim-finite-pair-localization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bud-localization)): Let $2a>0$ be regular. Then $a$ is regular, and restriction to the half-translation fixed locus induces an isomorphism $$H_K^*(X_{2a},M)[v^{-1}] \longrightarrow H_K^*(X_{2a}^{\tau},M)[v^{-1}].$$ It is an actual isomorphism in every sufficiently high degree, with the threshold allowed to depend on $a$. The same localization assertion holds for crossing pairs and respects their individual prime labels.* *For an ambient crossing at the $m$-th iterate of a prime, its actual $K$-group has uniformly bounded dimension in every degree, is zero below $I_m-A$, and maps isomorphically to its stabilized fixed target above $I_m+A$, after increasing the uniform constant $A$. For odd $m$ that target is zero; for even $m$ its root labels satisfy $\|l-I_{m/2}\|\le A$.
  - Explanation: After inverting the half-shift character, restriction to the half-translation fixed locus is an isomorphism, and actually so in high degrees.
- **claim-finite-cut-defect-bound** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-cut-budget)): There is a constant $C$ such that, whenever $2a>0$ is regular, $$\label{bud:linear-budget} \sum_{j\ge1}\Phi_j(a)\le C(1+a).$$
  - Explanation: The total defect potential over all degree cutoffs grows at most linearly in the cut.
- **claim-bounded-total-transients** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-bounded-transients)): Under the finite-prime-image assumption, there is a constant $C$, independent of the positive regular cut $a$, such that $$\label{trans:total-bound} T(a)\le C.$$
  - Explanation: Under finitely many prime images the total dimension of transient reflection classes is bounded uniformly in the cut.
- **claim-degree-gap-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-trans-degree-gaps)): There is a fixed margin $c$ such that, for every positive regular $a$ and every consecutive pair $s<s'$ in $\mathcal S$, the following statements hold.* 1. *In a present gap, $B_\infty^j\to B_a^j$ is injective for every $j\in\mathcal I_{s,s'}(a)$.* 2. *In an absent gap, $B_\infty^j\to B_a^j$ is zero for every $j\in\mathcal I_{s,s'}(a)$.* 3. *Whenever $j,j+1\in\mathcal I_{s,s'}(a)$, multiplication by $w$ induces an isomorphism $Q_a^j\to Q_a^{j+1}$.* *Moreover $B_a^j=0$ for $j>s_Na+c$.
  - Explanation: On degree gaps between consecutive slopes, restriction from the global group is injective or zero, and multiplication by the sign class is an isomorphism on transients.
- **claim-no-transients-in-gaps** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-trans-no-interior)): For all sufficiently large regular cuts $a$ with $2a$ regular, $Q_a^j=0$ on every gap $\mathcal I_{s,s'}(a)$ of Lemma <a href="#trans:degree-gaps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="trans:degree-gaps">34</a>.
  - Explanation: For large cuts no transient classes survive inside degree gaps, since a long string would exceed the linear defect budget.
- **claim-free-rank-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-act-module-rank)): Suppose that $2L>0$ is regular, and put $H=\bigoplus_j H_G^j(X_{2L},M)$. This is a finitely generated graded $\mathbb F_2[U]$-module. Its torsion submodule $\mathcal T$ is finite-dimensional and annihilated by a power of $U$. If $$t=\dim(\mathcal T/U\mathcal T)=\dim\ker(U\|_H), \qquad \rho=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb F_2[U]}(H/\mathcal T),$$ then $$\label{act:total-rank} \sum_j\dim B_{2L}^j=\rho+2t, \qquad \rho=\sum_j\dim B_L^j.$$
  - Explanation: The full-group cohomology at a finite cut is a finitely generated module over a polynomial ring, with finite torsion; total reflection dimension equals free rank plus twice the torsion.
- **claim-doubling-monotonicity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-doubling)): For every regular $2L>0$, $$\label{act:doubling} T(2L)\ge T(L).$$ If equality holds, then $$\label{act:odd-equality} h_{m,k}(2L)=b_{m,k}(2L) \qquad(m\ \text{odd},\ 0\le k\le d).$$
  - Explanation: The transient dimension does not decrease under doubling the cut, and equality forces every visible odd bottom to have a visible evaluation partner.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-analytic",
        "exact_quote": "The energy $E$ is smooth on $\\Lambda=H^1(S^1,S^n)$, satisfies the Palais–Smale condition, and has precisely the smooth closed geodesics and the constants as critical points. Sufficiently small energy sublevels retract equivariantly to $M$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-strips",
        "exact_quote": "If a closed bounded energy interval contains no critical value, the gradient flow gives equivariant sublevel equivalences across it. At regular cuts, open and closed sublevel pairs compute the same equivariant cohomology, naturally for inclusions and fixed loci.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-polygons",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $B<\\infty$. For every sufficiently large integer $N$, the energy sublevel $\\{E\\le B^2\\}$ retracts onto its broken-geodesic polygons with $N$ equal time cells. The retraction preserves every smaller energy sublevel. It commutes with reflection and all time shifts preserving the vertex grid, hence with $K$ when $N$ is even. Regular polygon sublevels through this bound are compact smooth manifolds with boundary. At every critical point in the band, polygon and Hilbert indices and nullities agree. Regular energy cuts are dense in the band, and their sublevels have finite CW models.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-prime-images",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-iteration",
        "exact_quote": "For each prime $c_i$, there is a number $\\Delta_i\\ge0$ such that $$\\label{eq:local-index}\n |I_{i,m+k}-I_{i,m}-I_{i,k}|\\le n,\\qquad\n |I_{i,m}-m\\Delta_i|\\le n.$$ There is an integer $D_i\\ge1$ such that, for $s=\\gcd(m,D_i)$, every null field at $c_i^m$ repeats a null field at $c_i^s$. Thus only finitely many root kernels occur. For even $m$, $$\\label{eq:local-even-index}\n |I_{i,m}-2I_{i,m/2}|\\le n.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-lens",
        "exact_quote": "Let a compact group $H$ act on a finite-dimensional or Hilbert manifold, and let $f$ be a smooth $H$-invariant function with a complete bounded $H$-equivariant strict descending flow $\\phi$. Suppose the critical set in a closed energy band is a finite disjoint union of compact sets at a single level $c$, and that $H$ permutes these sets. Assume that the energy drop is bounded below away from every neighborhood of those sets, and that the corresponding assertion holds on the annuli of nested neighborhoods. These conditions hold for the normalized energy flow of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:local-analytic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:local-analytic\">4</a>, and on compact polygon bands.*\n\n\n*For a common sufficiently large $T$ and sufficiently tight regular cuts $a<c<b$, set $$X=\\{f\\le b\\},\\quad X_-=\\{f\\le a\\},\\quad f_T=f\\circ\\phi_T,$$ $$\\label{eq:local-lens}\n Y=\\{x\\in X:f_T(x)\\le a\\},\\qquad\n N=\\{x\\in X:f_T(x)\\ge a\\},\\qquad Q=N\\cap Y.$$ There are natural relative cohomology isomorphisms, also after Borel construction, induced by $$\\label{eq:local-lens-maps}\n (X,X_-)\\longrightarrow(X,Y)\\longleftarrow(N,Q).$$ The lens splits into clopen pairs in the chosen disjoint critical neighborhoods. All maps commute with restriction to a fixed submanifold when the same flow is restricted there, with subgroup restriction, and with cup product by ambient absolute classes.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-local-control",
        "exact_quote": "Assume there are finitely many prime images. There are constants $A,B<\\infty$ such that every sufficiently tight crossing of a positive critical level has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *It decomposes into unoriented labels $(i,m)$ at that level. Let $\\mathcal P_{i,m}$ be the ordinary pair for one orientation. Then $$\\sum_j\\dim H^j(\\mathcal P_{i,m};\\mathbb F_2)\\le B,\\qquad\n     H^j(\\mathcal P_{i,m};\\mathbb F_2)=0\n                      \\quad\\text{if }|j-I_{i,m}|>A.$$*\n\n2.  *Its reflection block is induced from $\\mathcal P_{i,m}$; its $K$-block is induced from the $\\langle\\tau\\rangle$-action on that pair. For odd $m$ the fixed label is empty. For even $m$, the ordinary fixed pair has total rank at most $B$ and support $|j-I_{i,m/2}|\\le A$.*\n\n3.  *Full-to-fixed restriction respects the labels. These identifications are compatible with ambient maps and connecting homomorphisms.*\n\n4.  *Cup product by $e=\\mathop{\\mathrm{ev}}_0^*[S^n]^*\\in H_R^n(\\Lambda;\\mathbb F_2)$ vanishes on the reflection crossing, including each individual label.*\n\n*The constants depend on the metric and the finite prime list, and include all degenerate iterates and all zero-mean-index primes.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-local-control",
          "exact_quote": "Assume there are finitely many prime images. Then $$\\sum_j\\dim H^j(\\mathcal P_{i,m};\\mathbb F_2)\\le B,\\qquad\n     H^j(\\mathcal P_{i,m};\\mathbb F_2)=0\n                      \\quad\\text{if }|j-I_{i,m}|>A.$$*\n\n2.  *Its reflection block is induced from $\\mathcal P_{i,m}$; its $K$-block is induced from the $\\langle\\tau\\rangle$-action on that pair. For odd $m$ the fixed label is empty. For even $m$, the ordinary fixed pair has total rank at most $B$ and support $|j-I_{i,m/2}|\\le A$.*\n\n3.  *Full-to-fixed restriction respects the labels. These identifications are compatible with ambient maps and connecting homomorphisms.*\n\n4.  *Cup product by $e=\\mathop{\\mathrm{ev}}_0^*[S^n]^*\\in H_R^n(\\Lambda;\\mathbb F_2)$ vanishes on the reflection crossing, including each individual label.*\n\n*The constants depend on the metric and the finite prime list, and include all degenerate iterates and all zero-mean-index primes.",

For odd $m$ the fixed label is empty. For even $m$, the ordinary fixed pair has total rank at most $B$ and support $|j-I_{i,m/2}|\\le A$.*\n\n3.  *Full-to-fixed restriction respects the labels.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arclength",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact Riemannian manifold. There is a jointly continuous map $$\\Psi:[0,1]\\times H^1([0,1],M)\\longrightarrow H^1([0,1],M)$$ in the strong $H^1$ topology such that $\\Psi_0$ is the identity, $\\Psi_1\\gamma$ has constant speed, and every $\\Psi_u$ preserves length and endpoints. Constant paths and constant-speed paths are fixed. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:shortening-energy}\n E(\\Psi_u\\gamma)\n \\le (1-u)E(\\gamma)+u\\ell(\\gamma)^2\n \\le E(\\gamma).$$ The homotopy commutes with reversal. In particular, on loops it preserves evaluation at zero and the constant-loop subspace.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arclength",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact Riemannian manifold. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:shortening-energy}\n E(\\Psi_u\\gamma)\n \\le (1-u)E(\\gamma)+u\\ell(\\gamma)^2\n \\le E(\\gamma).$$ The homotopy commutes with reversal. In particular, on loops it preserves evaluation at zero and the constant-loop subspace.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-shortening-recipes",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $L_0<\\infty$ and let $M$ be compact and simply connected. Pairs of paths with common endpoints and Lipschitz constants at most $L_0$ admit a finite cover by relative $C^0$ neighborhoods in this path-pair space with the following property. On each neighborhood there is a relative-endpoint homotopy from the first path to the second, continuous in the strong $H^1$ topology of the input paths and the homotopy parameter.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-shortening-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be compact and simply connected. There are constants $C,D>0$, depending only on $M$, with the following property. Suppose a path is divided into finitely many pieces, each of which, on a unit interval, has Lipschitz constant at most one. Near any such path, within this space of piecewise Lipschitz paths, there is a relative-endpoint homotopy to a path of length at most $D$. At every stage its length is at most its original length plus $C$. The homotopy is continuous in $H^1$ on that neighborhood. The constants are independent of the number of pieces.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sphere-powers",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\alpha>0$ and $C_H$ depending on $g$ such that $$\\label{eq:cj-resonance}\n \\bigl|k-\\alpha\\operatorname{cr}(z)\\bigr|\\le C_H$$ for every nonzero homogeneous ordinary homology or cohomology class $z$ of degree $k$. For homology, $\\operatorname{cr}$ is the infimum of the levels $a$ carrying the class in $X_a=\\{F<a\\}$; for cohomology it is the supremum of the levels on which its restriction vanishes. Moreover, each ordinary homology and cohomology group in a fixed degree has dimension at most one. There are classes $$\\Theta\\in H_{3n-2}(\\Lambda),\\qquad\n \\omega\\in H^{n-1}(\\Lambda,M),\\qquad\n \\theta\\in H^{3n-2}(\\Lambda,M)$$ with $\\langle\\theta,\\Theta\\rangle=1$ such that, for every $m\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\Theta^{\\bullet m}|&=n+2md,&\n |\\omega^{\\circledast m}|&=(2m-1)d,\\label{eq:cj-power-degrees}\\\\\n \\bigl\\langle\\theta\\circledast\\omega^{\\circledast m},\n          \\Theta^{\\bullet(m+1)}\\bigr\\rangle&=1.\n \\label{eq:cj-power-pairing}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular these powers are nonzero, and $H_n(\\Lambda)=\\mathbb F_2\\,[M]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sphere-powers",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\alpha>0$ and $C_H$ depending on $g$ such that $$\\label{eq:cj-resonance}\n \\bigl|k-\\alpha\\operatorname{cr}(z)\\bigr|\\le C_H$$ for every nonzero homogeneous ordinary homology or cohomology class $z$ of degree $k$. For homology, $\\operatorname{cr}$ is the infimum of the levels $a$ carrying the class in $X_a=\\{F<a\\}$; for cohomology it is the supremum of the levels on which its restriction vanishes. Moreover, each ordinary homology and cohomology group in a fixed degree has dimension at most one. There are classes $$\\Theta\\in H_{3n-2}(\\Lambda),\\qquad\n \\omega\\in H^{n-1}(\\Lambda,M),\\qquad\n \\theta\\in H^{3n-2}(\\Lambda,M)$$ with $\\langle\\theta,\\Theta\\rangle=1$ such that, for every $m\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\Theta^{\\bullet m}|&=n+2md,&\n |\\omega^{\\circledast m}|&=(2m-1)d,\\label{eq:cj-power-degrees}\\\\\n \\bigl\\langle\\theta\\circledast\\omega^{\\circledast m},\n          \\Theta^{\\bullet(m+1)}\\bigr\\rangle&=1.\n \\label{eq:cj-power-pairing}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular these powers are nonzero, and $H_n(\\Lambda)=\\mathbb F_2\\,[M]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $\\alpha>0$ and $C_H$ depending on $g$ such that $$\\label{eq:cj-resonance}\n \\bigl|k-\\alpha\\operatorname{cr}(z)\\bigr|\\le C_H$$ for every nonzero homogeneous ordinary homology or cohomology class $z$ of degree $k$. For homology, $\\operatorname{cr}$ is the infimum of the levels $a$ carrying the class in $X_a=\\{F<a\\}$; for cohomology it is the supremum of the levels on which its restriction vanishes. Moreover, each ordinary homology and cohomology group in a fixed degree has dimension at most one. There are classes $$\\Theta\\in H_{3n-2}(\\Lambda),\\qquad\n \\omega\\in H^{n-1}(\\Lambda,M),\\qquad\n \\theta\\in H^{3n-2}(\\Lambda,M)$$ with $\\langle\\theta,\\Theta\\rangle=1$ such that, for every $m\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\Theta^{\\bullet m}|&=n+2md,&\n |\\omega^{\\circledast m}|&=(2m-1)d,\\label{eq:cj-power-degrees}\\\\\n \\bigl\\langle\\theta\\circledast\\omega^{\\circledast m},\n          \\Theta^{\\bullet(m+1)}\\bigr\\rangle&=1.\n \\label{eq:cj-power-pairing}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular these powers are nonzero, and $H_n(\\Lambda)=\\mathbb F_2\\,[M]$.",
      "explanation": "Resonance links degree and critical level on spheres, and powers of the loop-product and cohomology-product classes pair nontrivially.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cj-small-chains",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a pulled-back Thom cocycle, and let $h$ be a continuous map from its larger tube $O$ to a space carrying a cocycle $T$. Extend $h^*T$ by zero on simplices not wholly contained in $O$, and write $b=U\\smile\\widetilde{h^*T}$. Subdivide the finite parameter families factorwise so that a nonzero $U$-face forces its entire product rectangle into a fixed closed intermediate tube contained in $O$. Its preimage in each finite compact parameter domain is compact. Then $\\delta b=0$ on the shuffled simplices and every later face or image-preserving comparison prism in these rectangles.*\n\n*If $T$ vanishes on loops of length less than $L$, the subdivision may also ensure the following: a contributing term with a nonzero $U$-face and a nonzero $T$-face has $\\ell(h)\\ge L-\\varepsilon$ throughout its whole rectangle, for any fixed $\\varepsilon>0$. The same conclusion holds for the block and skip faces used by cup-one, and for all later image-preserving subdivision prisms.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cj-small-chains",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a pulled-back Thom cocycle, and let $h$ be a continuous map from its larger tube $O$ to a space carrying a cocycle $T$. Subdivide the finite parameter families factorwise so that a nonzero $U$-face forces its entire product rectangle into a fixed closed intermediate tube contained in $O$. Its preimage in each finite compact parameter domain is compact. Then $\\delta b=0$ on the shuffled simplices and every later face or image-preserving comparison prism in these rectangles.*\n\n*If $T$ vanishes on loops of length less than $L$, the subdivision may also ensure the following: a contributing term with a nonzero $U$-face and a nonzero $T$-face has $\\ell(h)\\ge L-\\varepsilon$ throughout its whole rectangle, for any fixed $\\varepsilon>0$. The same conclusion holds for the block and skip faces used by cup-one, and for all later image-preserving subdivision prisms.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cj-operations",
        "exact_quote": "The cut has degree $-q$, takes absolute cycles to absolute cycles, and preserves global null-homology. For a cycle in $\\mathcal A_a$ it is supported in $$\\label{eq:cj-cut-bound}\n \\mathcal A_{a-L+1}.$$ The same cut estimate holds on finite fillings. The join has degree $-n$ and satisfies the product boundary formula. On any prescribed finite collection of chains and fillings its radius can be chosen so that its output length is less than the sum of the two input length bounds plus $1$. All these statements are compatible with the refinement comparisons above.*\n\n\n*If $c$ is a fundamental cycle of constant loops, then $$\\label{eq:cj-chain-unit}\n \\mu(c,x)\\simeq x\\quad\\text{in }\\mathcal A_a$$ for every cycle $x$ in $\\mathcal A_a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-filtered-interchange",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a normalized cocycle of degree $p\\ge1$ vanishing on $\\mathcal A_L$, where $L>0$, and put $q=n+p-1$. Let $x$ and $y$ be arbitrary absolute cycles of respective degrees $j,N$ with $$j>n+p,\\qquad N>n+p,$$ supported in $\\mathcal A_a$ and $\\mathcal A_b$. With sufficiently small join radius and adequate refinements, $$\\label{eq:cj-interchange}\n \\mu(D_Tx,y)\\simeq\\mu(D_Ty,x)\n \\quad\\text{in }\\mathcal A_{a+b-L+c_{\\rm cj}},\\qquad c_{\\rm cj}=4.$$ Both sides have degree $j+N-q-n$. The constant $c_{\\rm cj}$ is independent of both degrees, the cocycle, and all length bounds. The join radius may be chosen for any additional prescribed finite collection of fillings without changing this constant.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-filtered-interchange",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a normalized cocycle of degree $p\\ge1$ vanishing on $\\mathcal A_L$, where $L>0$, and put $q=n+p-1$. With sufficiently small join radius and adequate refinements, $$\\label{eq:cj-interchange}\n \\mu(D_Tx,y)\\simeq\\mu(D_Ty,x)\n \\quad\\text{in }\\mathcal A_{a+b-L+c_{\\rm cj}},\\qquad c_{\\rm cj}=4.$$ Both sides have degree $j+N-q-n$. The constant $c_{\\rm cj}$ is independent of both degrees, the cocycle, and all length bounds. The join radius may be chosen for any additional prescribed finite collection of fillings without changing this constant.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $T$ be a normalized cocycle of degree $p\\ge1$ vanishing on $\\mathcal A_L$, where $L>0$, and put $q=n+p-1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cj-public-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "The join $\\mu$ induces $\\bullet$ on ordinary homology.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-absolute-unit",
        "exact_quote": "For the choices $S,T,l,L$ in <a href=\"#eq:cj-thresholds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cj-thresholds\">[eq:cj-thresholds]</a>, $$\\label{eq:cj-absolute-unit}\n [D_TS]=[M]\\quad\\text{in }H_n(\\Lambda).$$ Moreover $D_TS$ is supported in $\\mathcal A_{l-L+1}$, and there is a constant $B$ independent of $m$ such that it is homologous to a fundamental constant-loop cycle in $\\mathcal A_B$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-ordinary-death",
        "exact_quote": "For every smooth Riemannian metric on $S^n$, $n\\ge3$, there is a constant $D>0$ such that, for every integer $j\\ge0$ and every real $a>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a)\\longrightarrow H_j(\\Lambda)\\bigr)\n &\\subseteq\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a)\\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D})\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:ordinary-absolute}\\\\\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\\longrightarrow H_j(\\Lambda,M)\\bigr)\n &\\subseteq\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D},M)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:ordinary-relative}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same constant works at regular and nonregular levels. No assumption on the number of prime geodesic images is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-ordinary-death",
          "exact_quote": "For every smooth Riemannian metric on $S^n$, $n\\ge3$, there is a constant $D>0$ such that, for every integer $j\\ge0$ and every real $a>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a)\\longrightarrow H_j(\\Lambda)\\bigr)\n &\\subseteq\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a)\\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D})\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:ordinary-absolute}\\\\\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\\longrightarrow H_j(\\Lambda,M)\\bigr)\n &\\subseteq\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D},M)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:ordinary-relative}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same constant works at regular and nonregular levels. No assumption on the number of prime geodesic images is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gysin",
        "exact_quote": "For every $G$-pair $(Y,A)$, there are natural exact sequences $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_G^{j-2}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R} H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int} H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:standard-gysin}\\\\\n 0\\longrightarrow H_G^j(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_K}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int}H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eqv:weight-two-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For an $R$-pair and a $K$-pair, respectively, the sign-line sequences are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_R^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:reflection-gysin}\\\\\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_K^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots.\n \\label{eqv:half-shift-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The maps commute with maps of pairs and subgroup restriction wherever the latter applies, and satisfy the projection formula for absolute base classes.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gysin",
          "exact_quote": "For every $G$-pair $(Y,A)$, there are natural exact sequences $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_G^{j-2}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R} H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int} H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:standard-gysin}\\\\\n 0\\longrightarrow H_G^j(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_K}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int}H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eqv:weight-two-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For an $R$-pair and a $K$-pair, respectively, the sign-line sequences are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_R^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:reflection-gysin}\\\\\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_K^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots.\n \\label{eqv:half-shift-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The maps commute with maps of pairs and subgroup restriction wherever the latter applies, and satisfy the projection formula for absolute base classes.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $G$-pair $(Y,A)$, there are natural exact sequences $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_G^{j-2}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R} H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int} H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:standard-gysin}\\\\\n 0\\longrightarrow H_G^j(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_K}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int}H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eqv:weight-two-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For an $R$-pair and a $K$-pair, respectively, the sign-line sequences are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_R^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:reflection-gysin}\\\\\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_K^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots.\n \\label{eqv:half-shift-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The maps commute with maps of pairs and subgroup restriction wherever the latter applies, and satisfy the projection formula for absolute base classes.",
      "explanation": "Natural Gysin exact sequences relate Borel cohomology for the orthogonal group, reflection group and the four-group.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gysin",
          "exact_quote": "For every $G$-pair $(Y,A)$",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"

      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-global-generators",
        "exact_quote": "For $n\\ge3$, there are classes $$X_m\\in B_\\infty^{a_m}=H_R^{a_m}(\\Lambda,M),\\qquad m\\ge1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The classes $$\\xi_{m,k}=w^kX_m,\\qquad\n     \\eta_{m,k}=e\\,w^kX_m,\n     \\qquad m\\ge1,\\quad 0\\le k\\le d,$$ form a graded $\\mathbb F_2$-basis of $B_\\infty$. Their degrees are $$|\\xi_{m,k}|=a_m+k,\\qquad\n     |\\eta_{m,k}|=a_m+n+k.$$ We call the $\\xi_{m,k}$ the *bottoms* and the $\\eta_{m,k}$ the *tops* at stage $m$. Multiplication by $w^n$ annihilates $B_\\infty$.*\n\n2.  *Every $X_m$ is the restriction of a class $\\zeta_m\\in H_G^{a_m}(\\Lambda,M)$. If $m$ is odd, then $U^n\\zeta_m=0$. If $m$ is even, there is also a class $Y_m\\in H_G^{a_m+n}(\\Lambda,M)$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R Y_m=eX_m$.*\n\n3.  *For the round metric, let $Z_p$ be an open regular sublevel containing exactly the first $p$ positive critical manifolds, and choose $Z_0$ below the first one. For $H=R,G$, every crossing sequence for $(Z_p,Z_{m_0-1})$, $p\\ge m_0$, is short exact. A chosen class of the $m$-th crossing has a unique extension to $H_H^j(\\Lambda,Z_{m-1})$ whenever $j<a_{m+1}$, for $H=R,G$. The lifts in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> and the classes $X_m$ can be obtained from these relative extensions.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-global-generators",
          "exact_quote": "For $n\\ge3$, there are classes $$X_m\\in B_\\infty^{a_m}=H_R^{a_m}(\\Lambda,M),\\qquad m\\ge1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The classes $$\\xi_{m,k}=w^kX_m,\\qquad\n     \\eta_{m,k}=e\\,w^kX_m,\n     \\qquad m\\ge1,\\quad 0\\le k\\le d,$$ form a graded $\\mathbb F_2$-basis of $B_\\infty$. Their degrees are $$|\\xi_{m,k}|=a_m+k,\\qquad\n     |\\eta_{m,k}|=a_m+n+k.$$ We call the $\\xi_{m,k}$ the *bottoms* and the $\\eta_{m,k}$ the *tops* at stage $m$. Multiplication by $w^n$ annihilates $B_\\infty$.*\n\n2.  *Every $X_m$ is the restriction of a class $\\zeta_m\\in H_G^{a_m}(\\Lambda,M)$. If $m$ is odd, then $U^n\\zeta_m=0$. If $m$ is even, there is also a class $Y_m\\in H_G^{a_m+n}(\\Lambda,M)$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R Y_m=eX_m$.*\n\n3.  *For the round metric, let $Z_p$ be an open regular sublevel containing exactly the first $p$ positive critical manifolds, and choose $Z_0$ below the first one. For $H=R,G$, every crossing sequence for $(Z_p,Z_{m_0-1})$, $p\\ge m_0$, is short exact. A chosen class of the $m$-th crossing has a unique extension to $H_H^j(\\Lambda,Z_{m-1})$ whenever $j<a_{m+1}$, for $H=R,G$. The lifts in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> and the classes $X_m$ can be obtained from these relative extensions.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $n\\ge3$, there are classes $$X_m\\in B_\\infty^{a_m}=H_R^{a_m}(\\Lambda,M),\\qquad m\\ge1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The classes $$\\xi_{m,k}=w^kX_m,\\qquad\n     \\eta_{m,k}=e\\,w^kX_m,\n     \\qquad m\\ge1,\\quad 0\\le k\\le d,$$ form a graded $\\mathbb F_2$-basis of $B_\\infty$. Their degrees are $$|\\xi_{m,k}|=a_m+k,\\qquad\n     |\\eta_{m,k}|=a_m+n+k.$$ We call the $\\xi_{m,k}$ the *bottoms* and the $\\eta_{m,k}$ the *tops* at stage $m$. Multiplication by $w^n$ annihilates $B_\\infty$.*\n\n2.  *Every $X_m$ is the restriction of a class $\\zeta_m\\in H_G^{a_m}(\\Lambda,M)$. If $m$ is odd, then $U^n\\zeta_m=0$. If $m$ is even, there is also a class $Y_m\\in H_G^{a_m+n}(\\Lambda,M)$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R Y_m=eX_m$.*\n\n3.  *For the round metric, let $Z_p$ be an open regular sublevel containing exactly the first $p$ positive critical manifolds, and choose $Z_0$ below the first one. The lifts in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> and the classes $X_m$ can be obtained from these relative extensions.",
      "explanation": "Global reflection classes form a basis of bottoms and tops annihilated by a power of the sign class, with full-group lifts.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-eqv-round-attachment",
        "exact_quote": "The negative bundle of $C_m$ has rank $a_m$, and the kernel of the round Hessian is exactly $TC_m$.       },

The small sublevel $Z_0$ retracts $G$-equivariantly to $M$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-eqv-critical-modules",
        "exact_quote": "Define homogeneous polynomials $A_i\\in\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $$A_0=1,\\qquad A_1=w,\\qquad\n A_i=wA_{i-1}+UA_{i-2}\\quad(i\\ge2).\n \\label{eqv:grassmann-recurrence}$$ If $m$ is odd, the $G$-crossing is generated over $\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$, and $$A_{d+1}x_m=A_{d+2}x_m=0.\n \\label{eqv:grassmann-relations}$$ If $m$ is even, multiplication by $U$ is injective on the $G$-crossing, and that crossing is generated over $\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$ and a chosen class $y_m$ of degree $a_m+n$ whose reflection restriction is $ex_m$.",
        "source_id": "PM-345.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-eqv-critical-modules",
          "exact_quote": "Define homogeneous polynomials $A_i\\in\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $$A_0=1,\\qquad A_1=w,\\qquad\n A_i=wA_{i-1}+UA_{i-2}\\quad(i\\ge2).\n \\label{eqv:grassmann-recurrence}$$ If $m$ is odd, the $G$-crossing is generated over $\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$, and $$A_{d+1}x_m=A_{d+2}x_m=0.\n \\label{eqv:grassmann-relations}$$ If $m$ is even, multiplication by $U$ is injective on the $G$-crossing, and that crossing is generated over $\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$ and a chosen class $y_m$ of degree $a_m+n$ whose reflection restriction is $ex_m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Define homogeneous polynomials $A_i\\in\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $$A_0=1,\\qquad A_1=w,\\qquad\n A_i=wA_{i-1}+UA_{i-2}\\quad(i\\ge2).\n \\label{eqv:grassmann-recurrence}$$ If $m$ is odd, the $G$-crossing is generated over $\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$, and $$A_{d+1}x_m=A_{d+2}x_m=0.\n \\label{eqv:grassmann-relations}$$ If $m$ is even, multiplication by $U$ is injective on the $G$-crossing, and that crossing is generated over $\\mathbb F_2[w,U]$ by $x_m$ and a chosen class $y_m$ of degree $a_m+n$ whose reflection restriction is $ex_m$.",
      "explanation": "Odd crossings are cyclic modules with Grassmann relations; at even crossings the variable U acts injectively and two generators suffice.",

    {
      "conclusion": {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **prime closed geodesic** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A closed geodesic is *prime* if it traverses its periodic trajectory once.
- **regular cut** ([source](#sec:setup)): A positive cut $a$ is *regular* if $a^2$ is a regular value of $E$.
- **Borel equivariant cohomology** ([source](#sec:setup)): For an $H$-invariant pair $(Y,Z)$, let $$H_H^j(Y,Z)=H^j(EH\times_HY,EH\times_HZ;\mathbb F_2).$$
- **good pair** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Here a *good pair* means a closed pair whose second space is a strong deformation retract of an open neighborhood; its relative singular cohomology is the reduced cohomology of its pointed quotient.
- **bottoms and tops** ([source](#stmt-thm-global-generators)): We call the $\xi_{m,k}$ the *bottoms* and the $\eta_{m,k}$ the *tops* at stage $m$.
- **transient dimension T(a)** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Define $$T(a)=\dim\mathop{\mathrm{coker}}(B_\infty\longrightarrow B_a),$$ where the dimension is summed over all degrees.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-interchange-length-cost | additive length cost of the filtered interchange | = | $4$ | Independent of both degrees, the cocycle and all length bounds. |
| q-uniform-filling-increment | uniform filling increment D | = | $C_{\mathrm{gap}}+D_*+6$ | Depends only on the metric and the dimension. |
| q-bottom-degree | degree of the bottom class at stage m | = | $(2m-1)d$ | Stage index at least one. |
| q-index-mean-deviation | deviation of the iterate index from the mean-index line | ≤ | $n$ | All iterates m. |
| q-defect-budget | total defect potential over all degree cutoffs | ≤ | $C(1+a)$ | Constant depends on the metric and the finite prime list. |
| q-transient-bound | total transient dimension T(a) | ≤ | $C$ | All positive regular cuts. |
| q-doubling-inequality | transient dimension at the doubled cut | ≥ | $T(L)$ | Regular cuts with regular double. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-analytic-setup** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-analytic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-analytic): Hilbert-manifold structure, Palais-Smale compactness, normalized gradient flow -> complete equivariant flow and critical-point description
- **step-regular-strips** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-strips) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-strips): uniform energy decrease on regular bands -> sublevel equivalences across regular intervals
- **step-polygon-models** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-polygons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-polygons): replace central subintervals by minimizing segments -> finite-dimensional polygon models
- **step-prime-images** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-prime-images) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-prime-images): least period and uniqueness for the geodesic equation -> counting of prime images
- **step-iteration-index** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-iteration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-iteration): codimension-bounded restrictions of index forms and monodromy eigenvalue orders -> index estimates and finitely many root kernels
- **step-flow-lens** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-lens): trapping of flow segments and closed pasting -> natural crossing isomorphisms
- **step-normal-shift** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-shift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-shift): normal form splitting and relative Thom calculation -> degree-shifted rank equality
- **step-local-control** [claim](#stmt-thm-local-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-local-control): finitely many root types and evaluation into a punctured sphere -> uniform rank bounds and vanishing evaluation cup
- **step-arclength** [claim](#stmt-lem-arclength) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arclength): reparametrization by normalized arclength with convexity energy bound -> continuous constant-speed homotopy
- **step-fixed-degree-filling** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-degree-death) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-degree-death): local replacement, block shortening, colouring of simplices, bounded-level filling -> degree-dependent filling increment
- **step-sphere-resonance** [claim](#stmt-prop-sphere-powers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sphere-powers): resonance of Hingston and Rademacher and string-product powers -> degree-level relation and sphere powers
- **step-cut-join** [claim](#stmt-lem-cj-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cj-operations): supported cochain calculus applied to cut and join chains -> degree and length properties of the cut and join
- **step-interchange** [claim](#stmt-thm-filtered-interchange) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-filtered-interchange): three comparisons with vanishing second half, cup-one commutation and rotation symmetry -> interchange homology with fixed length cost
- **step-absolute-unit** [claim](#stmt-prop-absolute-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-absolute-unit): scalar comparison, interchange and nonvanishing of the join -> cut of the power equals the constant fundamental class
- **step-ordinary-death** [claim](#stmt-thm-ordinary-death) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-ordinary-death): degree reduction by cut, filling in fixed degree, join with sphere power to restore length -> degree-independent filling increment
- **step-gysin** [claim](#stmt-prop-gysin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gysin): relative Thom sequence for sphere bundles of representations -> natural Gysin sequences
- **step-global-generators** [claim](#stmt-thm-global-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eqv-interval-collapse): round crossings, critical-manifold cohomology, interval collapse and odd torsion -> global basis and full-group lifts
- **step-localization** [claim](#stmt-bud-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-localization): equivariant covering of the complement of the fixed locus and inversion of the half-shift class -> localization isomorphism and stable local ranges
- **step-defect-budget** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite-cut-budget) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-finite-cut-budget): exact-sequence diagram through each critical event with a potential function -> linear defect bound
- **step-degree-gaps** [claim](#stmt-trans-degree-gaps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-degree-gaps): ordinary death outside index bands plus sign-line Gysin sequences -> behaviour of restriction on degree gaps
- **step-no-interior** [claim](#stmt-trans-no-interior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-no-interior): a long string of transients gives quadratic discrepancy against the linear budget -> no transients inside degree gaps
- **step-bounded-transients** [claim](#stmt-thm-bounded-transients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-no-interior-2): band count and local rank bounds, including low degrees by ordinary death -> uniform bound on transient dimension
- **step-module-rank** [claim](#stmt-act-module-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-act-module-rank): structure of finitely generated graded modules over a polynomial ring -> total rank equals free rank plus twice torsion
- **step-doubling** [claim](#stmt-thm-doubling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-doubling): compare free rank, torsion and visible classes at a cut and its double -> monotonicity and odd equality condition
- **step-final-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-spheres) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-doubling-2): maximal bounded transient value propagates under doubling, first appearance of an odd bottom contradicts equality via vanishing evaluation cup -> infinitely many prime images for spheres of dimension at least three
- **step-finite-covers** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-doubling-3): lift geodesics through a finite covering and bound the fibre count -> finite-cover corollary
- **step-three-manifolds** [claim](#stmt-cor-three-manifolds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-three-manifolds): split by fundamental group: infinite case by Rademacher and Taimanov, finite case by geometrization and finite covers -> closed three-manifold corollary

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-ordinary-death",
          "exact_quote": "For every smooth Riemannian metric on $S^n$, $n\\ge3$, there is a constant $D>0$ such that, for every integer $j\\ge0$ and every real $a>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a)\\longrightarrow H_j(\\Lambda)\\bigr)\n &\\subseteq\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a)\\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D})\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:ordinary-absolute}\\\\\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\\longrightarrow H_j(\\Lambda,M)\\bigr)\n &\\subseteq\n \\ker\\bigl(H_j(X_a,M)\\longrightarrow H_j(X_{a+D},M)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:ordinary-relative}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same constant works at regular and nonregular levels.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-ordinary-death",
      "inputs": [
        "step-absolute-unit",
        "step-interchange",
        "step-fixed-degree-filling"
      ],
      "method": "degree reduction by cut, filling in fixed degree, join with sphere power to restore length",
      "output": "degree-independent filling increment",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-ordinary-death"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-gysin",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gysin",
          "exact_quote": "For every $G$-pair $(Y,A)$, there are natural exact sequences $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_G^{j-2}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_R} H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int} H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,U\\,} H_G^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:standard-gysin}\\\\\n 0\\longrightarrow H_G^j(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\mathop{\\mathrm{res}}_K}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\int}H_G^{j-1}(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eqv:weight-two-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For an $R$-pair and a $K$-pair, respectively, the sign-line sequences are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_R^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,w\\,}H_R^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots,\n \\label{eqv:reflection-gysin}\\\\\n \\cdots\\longrightarrow H_K^{j-1}(Y,A)\n &\\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_R^j(Y,A)\n \\longrightarrow H_K^j(Y,A)\n \\xrightarrow{\\,v\\,}H_K^{j+1}(Y,A)\\longrightarrow\\cdots.\n \\label{eqv:half-shift-gysin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The maps commute with maps of pairs and subgroup restriction wherever the latter applies, and satisfy the projection formula for absolute base classes.",
          "source_id": "PM-345.1"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-infinitely-many-prime-images-sphere**: On every standard smooth sphere of dimension at least two, every smooth Riemannian metric has infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise different images.
- **inv-bounded-transients-under-finite-assumption**: Under the assumption of finitely many prime images, the total dimension of the reflection classes at a finite cut that do not come from the whole loop space is bounded independently of the cut.
- **inv-prime-root-of-closed-geodesic**: Every nonconstant closed geodesic is an iterate of a prime geodesic, so images can be counted through prime roots.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-constructive-geodesic-finder**: Use the theorem as a procedure that locates or enumerates closed geodesics on a given sphere metric. -> The result is an existence statement proved by contradiction from a finite-image assumption; it gives no coordinates, search rule or location rate for the geodesics.
- **anti-use-other-manifolds**: Apply the conclusion to an arbitrary closed manifold in dimension other than three that has no finite spherical cover. -> The argument rests on sphere resonance and on finite spherical covers, or on three-manifold geometrization; nothing is claimed for other manifolds.
- **anti-use-bounded-transients-unconditional**: Quote the bounded-transient estimate as a fact about every metric on the sphere. -> The bound is derived under the finite-prime-image assumption, which the paper shows to be impossible, so it does not describe any actual metric.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements proved by contradiction from the assumption of finitely many prime images; they come with no finite procedure, explicit construction or computable bound that a program could execute. The quantities that appear are constants whose existence is asserted, not computed.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result is a non-constructive existence statement for closed geodesics, reached by contradiction from a finite-prime assumption. It gives no procedure that could be turned into code. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sphere theorem concerns infinitely many prime closed geodesic images for arbitrary smooth metrics. It provides no simulation scheme, no integrator and no error control for trajectories. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computational tool is specified. The statements describe homological invariants of loop spaces and a contradiction argument, not a checkable artefact. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument assumes finitely many prime images and derives a contradiction. It contains no finite step-by-step procedure, so no complexity can be stated. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polygon models with equal time cells are proof devices for retracting sublevels, not a stored data structure with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns circuits, parallel execution or hardware. The content is algebraic topology of loop spaces. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, primitive or protocol appears. Geodesic infinitude has no security reading. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning, estimation or optimisation of parameters occurs. The result is purely a statement in Riemannian geometry. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The energy functional on loops and its critical points are analysed through Morse theory, but no optimisation procedure or convergence guarantee is given. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, replication or consensus is involved. The paper contains only a geometric existence proof. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is foundational mathematics: it settles existence of infinitely many geometrically distinct prime closed geodesics on spheres and, via covers, on closed three-manifolds. It has no direct engineering construction. |

          "exact_quote": "For every regular $2L>0$, $$\\label{act:doubling}\n T(2L)\\ge T(L).$$ If equality holds, then $$\\label{act:odd-equality}\n h_{m,k}(2L)=b_{m,k}(2L)\n \\qquad(m\\ \\text{odd},\\ 0\\le k\\le d).$$",

The retraction preserves every smaller energy sublevel. It commutes with reflection and all time shifts preserving the vertex grid, hence with $K$ when $N$ is even. Regular polygon sublevels through this bound are compact smooth manifolds with boundary. At every critical point in the band, polygon and Hilbert indices and nullities agree. Regular energy cuts are dense in the band, and their sublevels have finite CW models.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-dimension-at-least-two** (hypothesis): The main sphere theorem is stated for the standard smooth sphere of dimension at least two; the higher-dimensional proof by contradiction is carried out for dimension at least three, with the two-sphere case taken from earlier work. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to manifolds that are neither standard spheres nor covered by one', 'Claiming a self-contained proof for the two-sphere inside this paper']
- **lim-finite-spherical-cover** (hypothesis): The finite-cover corollary needs a finite smooth cover diffeomorphic to a standard sphere of dimension at least two. Blocked use: ['Applying the corollary to a closed manifold with no finite spherical cover']
- **lim-three-manifold-scope** (hypothesis): The three-manifold corollary covers nonempty closed smooth three-manifolds only, orientable or not, and only the infinitude of images is asserted. Blocked use: ['Applying it to four-dimensional or higher closed manifolds', 'Reading it as a length-counting statement for finite fundamental group']
- **lim-finite-prime-assumption** (hypothesis): The local control, finite-cut budget, bounded transient and doubling results all assume finitely many prime images; this assumption is refuted in the end, so these results describe no actual metric. Blocked use: ['Quoting the bounded transient estimate as an unconditional fact about a metric on the sphere', 'Using the uniform local rank constants for a real metric']
- **lim-nonconstructive-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The final step argues by contradiction from a finiteness assumption; no procedure locating or counting geodesics, and no rate, is given. Blocked use: ['Using the result as a geodesic-finding algorithm', 'Extracting a growth rate of geodesic images from the proof']
- **lim-ordinary-death-dimension** (formal_scope): Uniform ordinary death is proved for every metric on the sphere of dimension at least three; the increment depends on the metric and the dimension, and no finite-prime hypothesis is required. Blocked use: ['Reading the filling increment as a universal number independent of the metric', 'Applying it to manifolds other than spheres of dimension at least three']
- **lim-constants-ineffective** (approximation): The constants in the defect budget and the transient bound are asserted to exist and depend on the metric and the finite prime list; no explicit value is given. Blocked use: ['Using these constants as numerical thresholds in computation']
- **lim-regular-cuts** (edge_case): Doubling, localization and the budget are stated at regular cuts whose double is regular; nonregular levels are handled only through nearby regular cuts. Blocked use: ['Applying the doubling inequality at a cut where the double is a critical value']
- **lim-polygon-subdivision** (hypothesis): The polygon retraction needs the number of time cells large enough for the chosen energy bound; coarse subdivisions give no such retraction. Blocked use: ['Using under-sampled broken-geodesic models to compute indices']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We resolve the sphere case of the closed-geodesic infinitude problem: every smooth Riemannian metric on the standard sphere <i>S</i><sup><i>n</i></sup>, <i>n</i> ≥ 2, has infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise distinct images. The two-sphere case is classical; the result in higher dimensions includes degenerate metrics. Using finite covers and theorems of Perelman and Rademacher–Taimanov, we also obtain the same conclusion for every smooth Riemannian metric on a nonempty closed smooth three-manifold.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The closed-geodesic infinitude problem asks whether every closed Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two has infinitely many geometrically distinct closed geodesics. A closed geodesic is *prime* if it traverses its periodic trajectory once. Geometric distinction means distinction of images: changing the initial point, reversing orientation, or taking an iterate produces no new image. A prime geodesic may have self-intersections. We resolve the sphere case of the infinitude problem.

<a id="stmt-thm-spheres"></a>
<div id="thm:spheres" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $n\ge2$ and every smooth Riemannian metric on the standard smooth sphere $S^n$, there are infinitely many prime closed geodesics with pairwise different images.*

</div>

Birkhoff developed a minimax construction for closed geodesics on surfaces of genus zero (Birkhoff 1917, sec. 17) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Birkhoff1917](#ref-Birkhoff1917)]. The existence of a nonconstant closed geodesic on every positive-dimensional closed Riemannian manifold goes back to Lyusternik and Fet (Lyusternik and Fet 1951) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[LyusternikFet1951](#ref-LyusternikFet1951)]. Infinitude requires separating new geodesics from repeated traversals of old ones. Bott’s iteration theory (Bott 1956) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Bott1956Iteration](#ref-Bott1956Iteration)] and the local Morse theory of Gromoll and Meyer (Gromoll and Meyer 1969) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GromollMeyer1969](#ref-GromollMeyer1969)] make that distinction quantitative. In particular, for a simply connected closed manifold, unbounded Betti numbers of the free loop space over a field force infinitely many geometrically distinct closed geodesics. Vigué-Poirrier and Sullivan (Vigué-Poirrier and Sullivan 1976) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ViguePoirrierSullivan1976](#ref-ViguePoirrierSullivan1976)] showed that rational cohomology requiring at least two algebra generators supplies this topological growth. Spheres have one-generator cohomology and bounded degreewise loop homology, so this criterion does not settle their infinitude problem.

The two-sphere case is classical.
